<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain had saturated the field before the match even began.  比赛还没开始，大雨已浸透了球场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bathe</td><td>/ˈbeɪð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>浸洗，沐浴；笼罩</td><td>bathe the wound<br>清洗伤口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Moonlight bathed the quiet valley in silver.  月光把寂静的山谷浸在银色中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 douse</td><td>/daʊs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扑灭（火或灯）；浇透</td><td>douse the flames<br>扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to douse the flames on the upper floors.  消防员整夜奋战，扑灭了高层的火焰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drench</td><td>/drentʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使湿透，浸透</td><td>drenched to the skin<br>浑身湿透</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sudden storm drenched us to the skin before we reached shelter.  一场突来的暴雨在我们找到避处前就把我们浇得浑身湿透。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soak</td><td>/soʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使…湿透</td><td>soak the beans overnight<br>把豆子浸泡一夜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soak the dried beans overnight before cooking them the next morning.  把干豆浸泡一夜，第二天早上再煮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 souse</td><td>/saʊs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>浸泡；浇透</td><td>souse herring in brine<br>把鲱鱼泡在盐水里</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She soused the vegetables in cold water before cooking them.  她做菜前把蔬菜泡在冷水里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bedraggle</del></td><td>/bɪˈdræɡəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使湿透，使脏乱</td><td>bedraggle one's clothes<br>把衣服弄得湿脏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain bedraggled her coat before she reached the door.  雨还没等她到门口就把外套淋得又湿又脏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wring out</td><td>/rɪŋ ˈaʊt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>拧干，榨出</td><td>wring out a wet towel<br>把湿毛巾拧干</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrung out the shirt and hung it on the line.  她把衬衫拧干后挂到绳上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>saturnine</b></td><td>/ˈsætərnaɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的，阴沉的</td><td>a saturnine temperament<br>阴沉的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His saturnine expression discouraged anyone from starting a cheerful conversation.  他阴沉的表情让人不敢开启轻松的话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bleak</td><td>/ˈbliːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（天气）阴冷的；荒凉的</td><td>a bleak winter day<br>阴冷的冬日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The moor looked bleak under grey November skies.  灰暗的十一月天空下，荒原显得阴冷荒凉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depressive</td><td>/dɪˈpresɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>抑郁的，令人沮丧的</td><td>a depressive mood<br>抑郁的情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long winters left him in a depressive state for months.  漫长的冬天让他数月处于抑郁状态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dismal</td><td>/ˈdɪzməl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>阴沉的，凄凉的</td><td>a dismal failure<br>彻底的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dismal gray sky matched everyone's mood.  阴沉灰暗的天空与众人的心情相符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreary</td><td>/ˈdrɪri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调乏味的</td><td>a dreary afternoon<br>沉闷的下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a dreary afternoon indoors watching rain streak the windows.  我们在室内度过一个沉闷的下午，看着雨水划过窗玻璃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miserable</td><td>/ˈmɪzərəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>痛苦的，凄惨的</td><td>feel utterly miserable<br>感到极其痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold rain made the whole camping trip miserable.  冰冷的雨让整个露营之行变得凄惨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 solemn</td><td>/ˈsɑːləm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>庄严的，郑重的</td><td>a solemn promise<br>郑重的承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceremony was solemn and lasted almost two hours.  仪式庄严肃穆，持续了近两个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jovial</td><td>/ˈdʒoʊviəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快的</td><td>a jovial host<br>愉快的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our jovial host kept the guests laughing throughout the long winter evening.  我们愉快的主人让客人们在漫长的冬夜里笑声不断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bright</td><td>/ˈbraɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快活的，开朗的；明亮的</td><td>a bright smile<br>开朗的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted every customer with a bright and genuine smile.  她用开朗真诚的笑容迎接每位顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cheerful</td><td>/ˈtʃɪrfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，愉快的</td><td>a cheerful greeting<br>愉快的问候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed cheerful even after the long delay.  即使长时间延误后他仍保持愉快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comforting</td><td>/ˈkʌmfərtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人安慰的，宽慰的</td><td>a comforting thought<br>一个令人安慰的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was comforting to know that help was only minutes away.  知道救援只有几分钟就到，这很让人安心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cordial</td><td>/ˈkɔːrdʒəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情友好的；令人振奋的</td><td>a cordial welcome<br>热情的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two leaders exchanged cordial greetings before the talks.  两位领导人在会谈前互致亲切的问候。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 festive</td><td>/ˈfestɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢乐的，节日的</td><td>a festive mood<br>欢乐的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole street took on a festive air by evening.  到傍晚整条街都染上了节日气息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heartwarming</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtˌwɔːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>暖心的，感人的</td><td>a heartwarming story<br>暖心的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their reunion after ten years was heartwarming to watch.  他们十年后的重逢看着令人暖心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sunshiny</td><td>/ˈsʌnˌʃaɪni/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>阳光灿烂的</td><td>a sunshiny morning<br>阳光灿烂的早晨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a sunshiny day, perfect for drying the laundry.  这是个阳光灿烂的日子，正适合晒衣服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>saunter</b></td><td>/ˈsɔːntər/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>闲逛，漫步</td><td>saunter down the street<br>沿街闲逛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sauntered into the office half an hour late.  他迟到半小时，悠然地走进办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amble</td><td>/ˈæmbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>漫步，闲逛</td><td>amble along the beach<br>沿海滩漫步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We ambled along the river until the sun went down.  我们沿着河边漫步，直到日落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ramble</td><td>/ˈræmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>漫谈，东拉西扯；漫步</td><td>ramble on about<br>喋喋不休地扯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rambled on about his childhood for a full hour.  他絮絮叨叨讲了整整一小时童年往事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 range</td><td>/ˈreɪndʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>排列，整齐排开；范围</td><td>range the books neatly<br>把书整齐排好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He ranged the bottles along the shelf by size and color.  他把瓶子按大小和颜色沿着架子排好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stroll</td><td>/ˈstroʊl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>散步，闲逛</td><td>take a stroll<br>去散散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They strolled through the old market after dinner.  晚饭后他们在老市场里闲逛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wander</td><td>/ˈwɑːndər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>漫游，闲逛；走神</td><td>wander around aimlessly<br>四处漫无目的闲逛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child wandered off while his parents were shopping.  孩子在父母购物时走开乱逛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 perambulate</del></td><td>/pəˈræmbjuleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>徒步穿越，走过</td><td>perambulate the old town<br>徒步走遍老城</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every evening the old couple perambulate the narrow streets of the town.  每天傍晚，那对老夫妻都在镇上的窄街间徒步走一圈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>savant</b></td><td>/sæˈvɑːnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>博学之士，学者</td><td>a quiet savant<br>沉静的博学之士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quiet savant spent forty years cataloguing insects in a small museum.  那位沉静的博学之士在小博物馆里花了四十年为昆虫编目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pundit</td><td>/ˈpʌndɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>权威人士，专家</td><td>a television pundit<br>电视上的评论专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every pundit predicted a close race and every one was wrong.  每位评论专家都预测选情接近，结果都错了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scholar</td><td>/ˈskɑːlər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>学者，博学之人</td><td>a distinguished scholar<br>杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture drew scholars from four different countries.  这场讲座吸引了四个国家的学者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unlearned person</td><td>/ʌnˈlɜːrnɪd ˈpɜːrsən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>没学问的人</td><td>advice from an unlearned person<br>来自无学问者的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even an unlearned person could see the argument was weak.  连没学问的人都能看出这论证很弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>savory</b></td><td>/ˈseɪvəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的，可口的</td><td>a savory dish<br>一道可口的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a savory pie made with mushrooms and herbs.  她端上一道用蘑菇和香草做的美味派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agreeable</td><td>/əˈɡriːəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉悦的；相一致的</td><td>an agreeable companion<br>一个讨人喜欢的同伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found him an agreeable companion on the long train journey.  她觉得他是长途火车旅程中一位讨人喜欢的同伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blessed</td><td>/ˈblesɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>幸福的，有福的</td><td>a blessed relief<br>极大的宽慰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the noise, the silence was a blessed relief.  喧闹过后，寂静是极大的宽慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 congenial</td><td>/kənˈdʒiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>和善的，友好的</td><td>a congenial host<br>一位和善的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our congenial host made every guest feel completely at home.  我们和善的主人让每位客人都感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delectable</td><td>/dɪˈlektəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的；令人愉悦的</td><td>a delectable dessert<br>美味的甜点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a delectable dessert made from summer berries.  她端上用夏日莓果做的美味甜点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delicious</td><td>/dɪˈlɪʃəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的，可口的</td><td>a delicious meal<br>一顿美味的饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soup was so delicious that nobody left a drop.  这汤太可口，没人剩下一滴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delightful</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，宜人的</td><td>a delightful surprise<br>令人愉快的惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a delightful evening by the lake.  我们在湖边度过了一个愉快的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dulcet</td><td>/ˈdʌlsɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，令人愉悦的</td><td>dulcet tones<br>悦耳的音调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dulcet tones calmed the restless children within a couple of minutes.  她悦耳的音调几分钟内就安抚了不安的孩子们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enjoyable</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒɔɪəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，有趣的</td><td>an enjoyable evening<br>愉快的一晚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We had a thoroughly enjoyable evening at the concert.  我们在音乐会上度过了非常愉快的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 felicitous</td><td>/fɪˈlɪsətəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，贴切的</td><td>a felicitous phrase<br>贴切的措辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The title is a felicitous choice for such a book.  这个书名对这样一本书来说是贴切的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grateful</td><td>/ˈɡreɪtfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人舒适的；感激的</td><td>grateful shade at noon<br>正午宜人的树荫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The travelers rested in the grateful shade of an old oak.  旅人们在老橡树宜人的树荫下歇息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 fying</del></td><td>/ˈfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>后缀：使……的（gratifying）</td><td>the ending -fying<br>词尾 -fying</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ending fying turns the root into an adjective here.  这里的词尾 fying 把词根变成形容词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heavenly</td><td>/ˈhevənli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天上的；美妙无比的</td><td>heavenly bodies<br>天体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Astronomers charted the heavenly bodies visible that clear night.  天文学家绘制了那个晴夜可见的天体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jolly</td><td>/ˈdʒɑːli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>欢乐的；开玩笑</td><td>a jolly atmosphere<br>欢快的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jolly innkeeper kept refilling everyone's glass and telling old stories.  这位欢快的店主不停给每人续杯，还讲着旧日的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luscious</td><td>/ˈlʌʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>甘美的，甜美诱人的</td><td>luscious ripe peaches<br>甘美的熟桃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market sold luscious peaches picked that morning.  市场上卖着当天早上采的甘美桃子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 palatable</td><td>/ˈpælətəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的；可接受的</td><td>a palatable compromise<br>令人满意的妥协</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chef made the healthy vegetables surprisingly palatable to the children.  厨师把健康的蔬菜做得让孩子们意外地喜欢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pleasurable</td><td>/ˈpleʒərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的</td><td>a pleasurable experience<br>愉快的经历</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reading aloud together became a pleasurable evening habit.  一起朗读成了愉快的晚间习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 satisfying</td><td>/ˈsætɪsˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人满意的；有说服力的</td><td>a satisfying explanation<br>令人满意的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one offered a satisfying answer to the reporter's question.  没人对记者的问题给出令人满意的答复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tasty</td><td>/ˈteɪsti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的；令人愉悦的</td><td>a tasty dish<br>一道美味的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cooked a tasty dish from the few vegetables left.  她用剩下的几样蔬菜做了一道美味的菜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 welcome</td><td>/ˈwelkəm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>欢迎，乐于接受</td><td>welcome the chance to help<br>乐于接受帮忙的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Residents welcomed the decision to reopen the public library.  居民们欢迎重新开放公共图书馆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disagreeable</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈɡriːəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的；脾气坏的</td><td>a disagreeable task<br>令人不快的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cleaning the drains was a thoroughly disagreeable job.  清理下水道是件极其讨人厌的活儿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 pleasureless</del></td><td>/ˈplɛʒərləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无乐趣的</td><td>a pleasureless routine<br>毫无乐趣的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of pleasureless work had drained his curiosity.  多年毫无乐趣的工作耗尽了他的好奇心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpalatable</td><td>/ʌnˈpælətəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难吃的；令人难以接受的</td><td>an unpalatable truth<br>难以接受的真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report contained several unpalatable truths about spending.  报告里有几条关于开支的难堪真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpleasant</td><td>/ʌnˈplezənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的，讨厌的</td><td>an unpleasant surprise<br>令人不快的意外</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was an unpleasant smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股难闻的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwelcome</td><td>/ʌnˈwelkəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不受欢迎的，讨厌的</td><td>unwelcome news<br>不受欢迎的消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrival brought unwelcome news about the closing factory.  他的到来带来了关于工厂停产的坏消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>savvy</b></td><td>/ˈsævi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>老练，机智；有见识的，精明能干的</td><td>political savvy<br>政治上的老练</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her political savvy helped the bill pass with almost no opposition.  她政治上的老练使那项议案几乎无异议通过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chops</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːps/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>本领，真功夫</td><td>have the chops for it<br>有做那件事的本领</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist clearly has the chops for concert work.  这位年轻钢琴家显然有开音乐会的真功夫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expertise</td><td>/ˌekspərˈtiːz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>专门知识，专长</td><td>technical expertise<br>技术专长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We hired her for her expertise in tax law.  我们因她在税法方面的专长而聘用她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proficiency</td><td>/prəˈfɪʃənsi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精通，熟练</td><td>proficiency in English<br>英语熟练</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The job requires proficiency in at least two foreign languages.  这份工作要求精通至少两门外语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 know-how</td><td>/ˈnoʊˌhaʊ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>实际技能，诀窍</td><td>practical know-how<br>实用的诀窍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building a boat takes patience and real know-how.  造一艘船需要耐心和真本事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 astute</td><td>/əˈstuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>机敏的，有洞察力的</td><td>an astute observer<br>敏锐的观察者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An astute observer would have noticed the tension in the room.  一个机敏的观察者会注意到房间里的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 canny</td><td>/ˈkæni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>精明的，聪明的</td><td>a canny investor<br>精明的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A canny investor sold his shares just before the crash.  一位精明的投资者在崩盘前卖掉了股票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clear-eyed</td><td>/ˌklɪr ˈaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>看得清楚的，冷静清醒的</td><td>a clear-eyed assessment<br>清醒的评估</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need a clear-eyed view of the risks before investing.  投资前我们需要对风险有清醒的认识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clear-sighted</td><td>/ˌklɪr ˈsaɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有眼光的，有洞察力的</td><td>a clear-sighted leader<br>有远见的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a clear-sighted planner could foresee the coming water shortage.  只有有眼光的规划者才能预见即将到来的缺水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hard-boiled</td><td>/ˌhɑːrd ˈbɔɪld/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>老练的，不易动情的</td><td>a hard-boiled detective<br>冷硬老练的侦探</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hard-boiled reporter never trusted an easy answer.  这位老练的记者从不相信轻易得来的答案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hardheaded</td><td>/ˈhɑːrdˌhedɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的；务实精明的</td><td>a hardheaded negotiator<br>强硬的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her hardheaded refusal to compromise stalled the talks for weeks.  她固执地拒绝妥协，使谈判停滞了数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heady</td><td>/ˈhedi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>使人兴奋的；精明的</td><td>a heady mix of ideas<br>令人兴奋的思想混合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Success brought a heady sense of freedom.  成功带来令人兴奋的自由感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sharp-witted</td><td>/ˈʃɑrp ˈwɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>机敏的，头脑敏锐的</td><td>a sharp-witted reply<br>机敏的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sharp-witted reply silenced the whole panel.  她机敏的回答让整个评审团哑然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 simplicity</td><td>/sɪmˈplɪsəti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>头脑简单；单纯质朴</td><td>childlike simplicity<br>孩童般的单纯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His simplicity in business matters cost him dearly.  他在商务上的头脑简单让他损失惨重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 tactlessness</del></td><td>/ˈtæktləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不得体，不会应对</td><td>sheer tactlessness<br>十足的不得体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His tactlessness at the funeral offended several relatives.  他在葬礼上的不得体冒犯了几位亲戚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inexperience</td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈspɪriəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏经验</td><td>youthful inexperience<br>年轻人的缺乏经验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her inexperience showed in the first few interviews.  她的缺乏经验在前几次面试中显露出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unknowing</td><td>/ʌnˈnoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不知情的，无意识的</td><td>an unknowing accomplice<br>不知情的帮凶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was an unknowing carrier of the virus for weeks.  她在数周内一直是不知情的病毒携带者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scads</b></td><td>/skædz/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>许多，大量</td><td>scads of money<br>大把的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film made scads of money in its first weekend alone.  这部电影仅首个周末就赚了大把的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abundance</td><td>/əˈbʌndəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>an abundance of fresh fruit<br>大量新鲜水果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market offered an abundance of fresh fruit at low prices.  市场以低价供应大量新鲜水果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plenitude</td><td>/ˈplɛnɪˌtud/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>充足，大量</td><td>a plenitude of evidence<br>充足的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The archive offered a plenitude of letters and diaries.  这个档案馆提供了大量书信和日记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profusion</td><td>/prəˈfjuːʒn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>a profusion of flowers<br>繁花盛开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A profusion of wildflowers covered the hillside after the spring rains.  春雨过后，山坡上开满了大量野花。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slew</td><td>/sluː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，许多</td><td>a slew of problems<br>一大堆问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new policy generated a slew of complaints from local business owners.  新政策引来当地商户的一大堆抱怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spate</td><td>/speɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量</td><td>a spate of complaints<br>大量投诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The store received a spate of complaints about the faulty heaters.  商店收到大量关于取暖器故障的投诉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wealth</td><td>/ˈwelθ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，丰富；财富</td><td>a wealth of experience<br>丰富的经验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old editor brought a wealth of experience to the team.  这位老编辑给团队带来了丰富的经验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 paucity</td><td>/ˈpɔːsəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，缺乏</td><td>a paucity of data<br>数据匮乏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paucity of reliable data made the researchers cautious about conclusions.  可靠数据的匮乏使研究者对结论十分谨慎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scant</b></td><td>/skænt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不足的，缺乏的</td><td>scant attention<br>很少的关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report paid scant attention to the concerns of local residents.  报告对当地居民的关切几乎没有给予关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 exiguous</del></td><td>/ɪɡˈzɪɡjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极少的，微薄的</td><td>an exiguous budget<br>少得可怜的预算</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They survived a whole year on exiguous rations of rice.  他们靠少得可怜的米粮过了整整一年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 niggardly</td><td>/ˈnɪɡərdli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，少得可怜的</td><td>a niggardly raise<br>少得可怜的加薪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered a niggardly raise after record profits.  在创纪录盈利后公司给了少得可怜的加薪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scarce</td><td>/ˈskers/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀缺的，不足的</td><td>in scarce supply<br>供应短缺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fresh water became scarce during the drought.  旱灾期间淡水变得稀缺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sparse</td><td>/spɑːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀疏的，稀少的</td><td>sparse vegetation<br>稀疏的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vegetation grows sparse as the trail climbs above the tree line.  小径爬上林线以上后，植被变得稀疏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stingy</td><td>/ˈstɪndʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>小气的，吝啬的</td><td>stingy with money<br>在钱上很吝啬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is stingy with money but surprisingly generous with his time.  他在钱上吝啬，在时间上却出奇大方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considerable</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（数量上）可观（而值得注意）的</td><td>a considerable sum<br>一笔可观的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The renovation required a considerable amount of money and nearly two years of work.  这次翻修耗费了可观的资金和近两年的工期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 copious</td><td>/ˈkoʊpiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，丰富的</td><td>take copious notes<br>做大量笔记</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took copious notes during every lecture and reread them each night.  她每堂课都记大量笔记，并在每晚重读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 voluminous</td><td>/vəˈluːmɪnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卷数多的，大量的，庞大的</td><td>voluminous records<br>卷帙浩繁的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer arrived with voluminous records of every payment ever made.  律师带着关于每一笔付款的浩繁记录到场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 profuse</td><td>/prəˈfjuːs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，丰富的</td><td>profuse apologies<br>再三的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered profuse apologies for arriving so late.  他为迟到再三道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 myriad</td><td>/ˈmɪriəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无限的，大量的</td><td>myriad possibilities<br>无数的可能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new technology opens myriad possibilities for teaching students in remote villages.  这项新技术为教偏远村庄的学生打开了无数可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scathing</b></td><td>/ˈskeɪðɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖酸刻薄的，严厉的</td><td>a scathing attack<br>尖刻的攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a scathing remark about his sloppy research.  她对他草率的研究作了一句尖刻的评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acerbic</td><td>/əˈsɜːrbɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（语调）尖酸的，刻薄的</td><td>acerbic wit<br>尖刻的机智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reviewer is famous for her acerbic comments on new plays.  这位评论家以对新剧的尖酸评论闻名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acid</td><td>/ˈæsəd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>尖刻的；酸的；酸</td><td>an acid remark<br>一句尖刻的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her acid comment about his writing silenced the whole table.  她对他文章的尖刻评论让全桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acrid</td><td>/ˈækrɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>刻薄的；辛辣刺鼻的</td><td>acrid smoke<br>刺鼻的烟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An acrid smell of burning plastic filled the hallway.  一股塑料烧焦的刺鼻气味充满了走廊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pungent</td><td>/ˈpʌndʒənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言辞）辛辣尖刻的；刺鼻的</td><td>pungent criticism<br>辛辣的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pungent criticism of the report left everyone at the table silent.  他对这份报告辛辣的批评让全桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scalding</td><td>/ˈskɔːldɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>滚烫的；（言辞）严厉的</td><td>scalding criticism<br>严厉的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic wrote a scalding review of the opening night.  评论家为首演之夜写了一篇严厉的评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snarky</td><td>/ˈsnɑːrki/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>讥讽的，尖酸的</td><td>a snarky comment<br>尖酸的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her snarky reply ended the conversation at once.  她讥讽的回答立刻结束了谈话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tart</td><td>/ˈtɑːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；酸的</td><td>a tart reply<br>尖刻的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her tart reply silenced everyone at the dinner table.  她尖刻的回答让餐桌上所有人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 polite</td><td>/pəˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有礼貌的，客气的</td><td>make polite conversation<br>客套地寒暄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is polite to greet your neighbors in the morning.  早上向邻居问好是有礼貌的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm compliment</td><td>/kɑːm ˈkɑːmpləmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平和的赞美</td><td>offer a calm compliment<br>给出平和的赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Instead of mockery he gave a calm compliment on her work.  他没有嘲讽，而是平和地赞美了她的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>schism</b></td><td>/ˈskɪzəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，派系对立</td><td>a bitter schism<br>严重的分裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bitter schism split the party into two hostile factions.  一场激烈的分裂把该党撕成两个敌对派系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conflict</td><td>/ˈkɑːnflɪkt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冲突，矛盾；抵触</td><td>a conflict of interest<br>利益冲突</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge withdrew because of a possible conflict of interest.  法官因可能存在利益冲突而回避。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discordance</td><td>/dɪsˈkɔːrdəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不一致，不和</td><td>discordance between reports<br>各报告之间的矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a clear discordance between the two witnesses' accounts.  两位证人的叙述之间存在明显不一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disharmony</td><td>/dɪsˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>不和谐，不一致</td><td>racial disharmony<br>种族间的不和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of disharmony finally split the once famous band.  多年的不和最终使这支曾经著名的乐队解散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissidence</td><td>/ˈdɪsədəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>异议，不同意</td><td>political dissidence<br>政治异见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime punished even mild dissidence among its writers.  该政权连作家中温和的异见也要惩罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissonance</td><td>/ˈdɪsənəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（音调）不和谐，刺耳；不一致，分歧</td><td>cognitive dissonance<br>认知失调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cognitive dissonance arises when our actions contradict the beliefs we hold.  当行为与所持信念矛盾时，认知失调便产生了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disunion</td><td>/dɪsˈjuːnjən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，不和</td><td>the threat of disunion<br>分裂的威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate over slavery pushed the nation toward disunion.  关于奴隶制的争论把这个国家推向分裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 friction</td><td>/ˈfrɪkʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>摩擦；冲突，不和</td><td>friction between them<br>他们之间的摩擦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant friction between the two managers slowed the whole project.  两位经理之间不断的摩擦拖慢了整个项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strife</td><td>/ˈstraɪf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>冲突，争斗，不和</td><td>civil strife<br>内乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of political strife left the country deeply exhausted.  多年的政治纷争使这个国家极度疲惫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accord</td><td>/əˈkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>一致，符合；授予</td><td>in accord with<br>与…一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His actions were in accord with the policy he announced.  他的行动与他宣布的政策一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agreement</td><td>/əˈɡriːmənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，同意；协议</td><td>come to an agreement<br>达成协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two firms signed an agreement to share research costs.  两家公司签署协议分担研究费用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concord</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，和睦</td><td>live in concord<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty brought decades of concord between the two states.  该条约给两国带来数十年的和睦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmony</td><td>/ˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和谐，融洽</td><td>live in harmony with<br>与…和谐共处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two communities have lived in harmony for generations.  这两个社区世代和睦相处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peace</td><td>/ˈpiːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和平，和睦；平静</td><td>live in peace<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two villages have lived in peace for over a century.  这两个村庄和睦相处已逾一个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>scintillate</b></del></td><td>/ˈsɪntɪleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闪耀</td><td>scintillate with wit<br>闪耀着才智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essays scintillate with wit even when the subject seems dull.  即使题目乏味，她的文章也闪耀着才智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gleam</td><td>/ɡliːm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>闪光</td><td>a gleam of hope<br>一线希望之光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single candle gleamed in the window of the abandoned farmhouse.  一支蜡烛在废弃农舍的窗口闪着光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glimmer</td><td>/ˈɡlɪmər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，一丝；微光</td><td>a glimmer of hope<br>一丝希望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letter offered a faint glimmer of hope.  那封信带来一丝微弱的希望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glisten</td><td>/ˈɡlɪsn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闪光</td><td>glisten with dew<br>因露水而闪亮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wet leaves glistened in the early morning sunlight after the storm.  暴风雨后，湿漉漉的叶子在清晨阳光下闪闪发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 glister</del></td><td>/ˈɡlɪstər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闪光，闪耀</td><td>glister in the sun<br>在阳光下闪耀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All that glisters is not gold, as the proverb warns.  正如谚语所警示，闪光的并非都是金子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luster</td><td>/ˈlʌstər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>光泽，光彩</td><td>lose its luster<br>失去光泽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careful polishing restored the luster of the old silver bowl.  仔细打磨恢复了这只旧银碗的光泽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sparkle</td><td>/ˈspɑːrkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>闪耀，闪亮；生气</td><td>eyes sparkle with joy<br>双眼闪着喜悦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her eyes sparkled when she finally opened the present.  她终于打开礼物时眼睛闪闪发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 winkle</td><td>/ˈwɪŋkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>费力弄出，挖出；闪烁</td><td>winkle out the truth<br>把真相挖出来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters slowly winkled out the truth about the secret land deal.  记者们一点点挖出了那笔秘密土地交易的真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 foolly</del></td><td>/ˈfuːli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>副词</td><td>愚蠢地，糊涂地</td><td>act foolly in haste<br>匆忙中行事糊涂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spoke foolly and regretted it within minutes.  他说话糊涂，几分钟内就后悔了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>seditious</b></td><td>/sɪˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>煽动性的，意在制造混乱的</td><td>seditious pamphlets<br>煽动性的小册子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soldiers found seditious pamphlets hidden beneath the floor of the barn.  士兵们在谷仓地板下发现了藏着的煽动性小册子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 agitational</del></td><td>/ˌædʒɪˈteɪʃənl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>煽动性的</td><td>agitational propaganda<br>煽动性的宣传</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The newspaper was closed for printing agitational articles.  这家报纸因刊印煽动性文章而被查封。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incendiary</td><td>/ɪnˈsendieri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>煽动者；纵火者</td><td>a political incendiary<br>政治煽动者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government labeled the anonymous pamphleteer a dangerous incendiary.  政府把这位匿名小册子作者称为危险的煽动者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 instigative</del></td><td>/ˈɪnstɪɡeɪtɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>煽动性的</td><td>instigative remarks<br>煽动性的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His instigative speech stirred the crowd into open revolt.  他煽动性的演讲激起人群公开反叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 provocative</td><td>/proʊˈvɑːkətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>煽动性的，挑拨的</td><td>a provocative speech<br>煽动性的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The article was deliberately provocative to attract attention.  这篇文章故意挑拨，以吸引注意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conciliatory</td><td>/kənˈsɪliəˌtɔːri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安抚的，调和的</td><td>a conciliatory gesture<br>和解的姿态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He adopted a conciliatory tone after the heated debate.  激烈辩论之后他改用了安抚的语气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consoling</td><td>/kənˈsoʊlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安慰的，慰藉的</td><td>a consoling embrace<br>一个安慰的拥抱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered a few consoling words before leaving the hospital room.  他在离开病房前说了几句安慰的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacific</td><td>/pəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平和的，爱好和平的</td><td>a pacific temperament<br>平和的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He settled the quarrel with a few pacific words.  他用几句平和的话平息了争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>scission</b></del></td><td>/ˈsɪʒən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>切断，分离，分裂</td><td>a clean scission<br>干净的切断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty produced a clean scission between the two neighboring provinces.  那份条约使两个相邻省份彻底分离。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bifurcation</td><td>/ˌbaɪfərˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分岔，一分为二</td><td>the bifurcation of the river<br>河流的分岔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sharp bifurcation of the path confused the hikers.  小径突然分岔，把徒步者弄糊涂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cleavage</td><td>/ˈkliːvədʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，裂开</td><td>a deep political cleavage<br>深刻的政治分裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A deep cleavage split the party into two hostile wings.  一道深刻的裂痕把该党分成两个敌对派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissolution</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈluːʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>解散，分解</td><td>the dissolution of parliament<br>议会的解散</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dissolution of the company left hundreds jobless.  公司解散让数百人失业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 division</td><td>/dɪˈvɪʒən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，不和；分开，部门</td><td>deep division over policy<br>政策上的深刻分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is deep division within the committee about the budget.  委员会内部在预算上存在深刻分歧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partition</td><td>/pɑːrˈtɪʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>分割，划分</td><td>the partition of the estate<br>地产的分割</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brothers argued for years over the partition of the family land.  兄弟们为家族土地的分割争执了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 schism</td><td>/ˈskɪzəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，派系对立</td><td>a bitter schism<br>严重的分裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bitter schism split the party into two hostile factions.  一场激烈的分裂把该党撕成两个敌对派系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sundering</td><td>/ˈsʌndərɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分离，割裂</td><td>the sundering of old ties<br>旧关系的割裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The war brought a painful sundering of old family ties.  战争带来了旧日家族关系的痛苦割裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unification</td><td>/ˌjuːnəfəˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>统一，联合</td><td>the unification of the country<br>国家的统一</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty prepared the way for the unification of the states.  这项条约为各邦统一铺平了道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 union</td><td>/ˈjuːnjən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>联合，结合；工会</td><td>a lasting union of states<br>各州的持久联合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two colonies formed a lasting union after long talks.  两块殖民地经长期谈判形成持久联合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scoff</b></td><td>/skɔːf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑</td><td>scoff at the idea<br>嘲笑这个想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues used to scoff at the idea of working from home.  同事们过去常嘲笑在家办公的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scorch</b></td><td>/skɔːrtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>炙烤，烘干</td><td>scorch the grass brown<br>把草烤成褐色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blazing sun scorched the grass brown within a single week.  烈日一周之内就把草烤成褐色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dehydrate</td><td>/diːˈhaɪdreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>去除水分，使干燥；使失去活力</td><td>dehydrate fruit for storage<br>把水果脱水储存</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Campers dehydrate fruit and meat so the food lasts for weeks.  露营者把水果和肉脱水，好让食物保存数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 desiccate</del></td><td>/ˈdesɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使干燥，使脱水</td><td>desiccate the soil completely<br>使土壤完全干透</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months without rain desiccated the fields and killed the young corn.  数月无雨使田地干透，幼小的玉米也枯死了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 parch</del></td><td>/pɑːrtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>烤干，使干透</td><td>parch the dry earth<br>把干土烤裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks without rain had parched the fields into cracked brown dust.  数周无雨已把田地烤得干裂成褐色的尘土。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sear</td><td>/ˈsɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>烤焦，烧灼；使枯萎</td><td>sear the meat quickly<br>快速把肉煎焦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sear the meat quickly on both sides before roasting it.  烤之前先把肉两面快速煎焦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 singe</td><td>/sɪndʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻微烧焦</td><td>singe the hair<br>把头发烧焦一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stood too close to the candle and singed her long hair.  她站得离蜡烛太近，把长发烧焦了一点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dampen</td><td>/ˈdæmpən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，压抑（感情、精力等），泼冷水</td><td>dampen his enthusiasm<br>打击他的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Losing the first game did nothing to dampen the team's spirits.  首场失利丝毫没有打击球队的士气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hydrate</td><td>/ˈhaɪˌdreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使含水，补水</td><td>hydrate the skin thoroughly<br>给皮肤充分补水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drink water regularly to hydrate your body during long runs.  长跑时要定时喝水给身体补水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wash</td><td>/ˈwɑːʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>冲洗，洗涤</td><td>wash away the dirt<br>冲掉污垢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain washed away the dirt from the steps.  大雨把台阶上的污垢冲掉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 water</td><td>/ˈwɔːtər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>给…浇水，灌溉</td><td>water the garden<br>给花园浇水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He waters the vegetable beds every morning before sunrise.  他每天日出前给菜地浇水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wet</td><td>/ˈwet/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>湿的，潮的；弄湿</td><td>soaking wet clothes<br>湿透的衣服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My shoes got wet crossing the flooded street.  我的鞋在穿过被淹的街道时湿了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scorn</b></td><td>/skɔːrn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>轻蔑，鄙视，不屑</td><td>scorn an offer<br>不屑接受提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He scorned the idea of asking his parents for money.  他不屑于向父母要钱这个想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despise</td><td>/dɪˈspaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鄙视，极其不喜欢</td><td>despise a coward<br>鄙视懦夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She despised him for abandoning his closest friends.  她鄙视他抛弃至交的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flout</td><td>/flaʊt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>嘲弄性不理会，蔑视</td><td>flout the rules<br>蔑视规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drivers who flout the rules face heavy fines this year.  今年蔑视规则的司机将面临重罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 contemn</del></td><td>/kənˈtem/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻视，蔑视</td><td>contemn the rules<br>蔑视规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seemed to contemn every tradition the school held dear.  他似乎蔑视学校珍视的每一项传统。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disdain</td><td>/dɪsˈdeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>轻视，鄙视</td><td>disdain to answer<br>不屑于回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She disdained the flattery of her ambitious colleagues.  她鄙视那些野心勃勃的同事的奉承。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 look down</td><td>/ˈlʊk ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>轻视，看不起</td><td>look down on him<br>看不起他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She never looked down on people with less education.  她从不看不起受教育较少的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 adulate</del></td><td>/ˈædʒəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>极度谄媚，拍马屁</td><td>adulate a celebrity<br>对名人极力吹捧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters adulated the young singer after her first hit.  记者们在这位年轻歌手第一首热门歌后对她大加吹捧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scotch</b></td><td>/skɑːtʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>停止</td><td>scotch a rumor<br>制止一则谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prime minister moved quickly to scotch rumors about her illness.  首相迅速出面平息关于她生病的谣言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hinder</td><td>/ˈhɪndər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍，妨碍</td><td>hinder progress<br>妨碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poor lighting hindered the search for survivors.  照明不足妨碍了对生还者的搜寻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thwart</td><td>/θwɔːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，挫败</td><td>thwart an attempt<br>挫败一次尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Alert guards thwarted the escape attempt.  警觉的守卫挫败了这次越狱企图。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scour</b></td><td>/ˈskaʊər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>搜查</td><td>scour the countryside<br>搜遍乡间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police continued to scour the countryside for the missing hiker.  警方继续在乡间搜寻失踪的徒步者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comb</td><td>/ˈkoʊm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>彻底搜查；梳理</td><td>comb the area for clues<br>在该地区搜寻线索</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police combed the woods for the missing child.  警方在林中彻底搜寻失踪的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dig through</td><td>/ˈdɪɡ θruː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>翻查，挖掘查找</td><td>dig through old files<br>翻查旧档案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dug through boxes of letters to find the date.  她翻遍一箱箱信件来找那个日期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dredge</td><td>/ˈdredʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>挖掘搜寻；疏浚</td><td>dredge up the past<br>把过去翻挖出来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters dredged up records from twenty years ago.  记者翻挖出二十年前的记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hunt through</td><td>/ˈhʌnt θruː/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>搜寻，翻找</td><td>hunt through the drawers<br>翻找抽屉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He hunted through his pockets for the missing ticket.  他翻遍口袋找那张不见的票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rake</td><td>/ˈreɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>搜寻，细查；耙</td><td>rake through the files<br>翻查这些档案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators raked through years of email records.  调查人员细查了多年的邮件记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ransack</td><td>/ˈrænˌsæk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底搜查；掠夺</td><td>ransack the room<br>把房间翻个底朝天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thieves ransacked the office looking for cash.  窃贼把办公室翻遍寻找现金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rifle</td><td>/ˈraɪfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>翻查搜寻；偷窃</td><td>rifle through a drawer<br>翻查抽屉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rifled through the papers until he found the deed.  他翻遍文件直到找到那份契约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rummage</td><td>/ˈrʌmɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>翻找，搜寻</td><td>rummage in the attic<br>在阁楼里翻找</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She rummaged in her bag for a pen.  她在包里翻找一支笔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sort through</td><td>/ˈsɔːrt θruː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逐一翻查整理</td><td>sort through old photos<br>翻看整理旧照片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We sorted through the boxes to find his diploma.  我们逐一翻查箱子找他的毕业证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 troll</td><td>/ˈtroʊl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>搜寻，寻觅</td><td>troll for information<br>四处搜寻信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters trolled the archives for forgotten scandals.  记者在档案中搜寻被遗忘的丑闻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scowl</b></td><td>/skaʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>皱眉（表现出不高兴）</td><td>scowl at the noise<br>对噪音皱眉不满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The librarian began to scowl at the students whispering near the shelves.  图书馆员开始对书架旁窃窃私语的学生皱眉不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frown</td><td>/ˈfraʊn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>不悦，不赞成；皱眉</td><td>frown on the practice<br>不赞成这种做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most universities frown on students sharing their assignments.  大多数大学不赞成学生互相分享作业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pout</td><td>/paʊt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（尤指撅嘴或板着脸）表示不悦</td><td>pout in silence<br>板着脸不出声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She began to pout when her parents refused to buy the toy.  父母不肯买那个玩具时，她开始撅嘴表示不悦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glare</td><td>/ˈɡler/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>怒视，瞪眼</td><td>glare at the driver<br>怒视那个司机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She glared at him until he put the phone away.  她怒视着他，直到他收起手机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gloom</td><td>/ˈɡluːm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>阴沉，忧郁</td><td>an air of gloom<br>一种阴郁的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A deep gloom settled over the team after the loss.  失利后一股深深的阴郁笼罩全队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glower</td><td>/ˈɡlaʊər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>怒目而视</td><td>glower at an opponent<br>怒目瞪视对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach glowered at the referee but said nothing about the call.  教练怒目瞪着裁判，却没对那次判罚说什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lower</td><td>/ˈloʊər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>降低，减少；较低的</td><td>lower the temperature<br>降低温度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bank lowered interest rates twice in one year.  银行一年内两次降低利率。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 beam</td><td>/ˈbiːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>笑容满面；光束</td><td>beam with pride<br>满脸自豪地微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The father beamed with pride as his daughter walked onstage.  女儿走上台时父亲笑得满脸自豪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grin</td><td>/ɡrɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>咧嘴笑，咧嘴笑着表示</td><td>grin from ear to ear<br>笑得合不上嘴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grinned broadly when the judges announced his name as the winner.  当评委宣布他获胜时，他开心地咧嘴大笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smile</td><td>/ˈsmaɪl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>微笑</td><td>smile warmly at someone<br>对某人温暖地微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She smiled at the child who had dropped his toy.  她对掉了玩具的孩子微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scrappy</b></td><td>/ˈskræpi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，好吵架的</td><td>a scrappy little dog<br>爱打架的小狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A scrappy little dog guarded the yard and challenged every visitor.  一只爱打架的小狗看守院子，向每位来客叫阵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggressive</td><td>/əˈɡresɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的；积极进取的，强有力的</td><td>an aggressive marketing campaign<br>强势的营销活动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog became aggressive whenever strangers approached the gate.  只要有陌生人靠近大门，这只狗就变得有攻击性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assaultive</td><td>/əˈsɔːltɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好攻击的，暴力的</td><td>assaultive behavior<br>暴力攻击行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient's assaultive behavior required constant supervision by trained staff.  这名病人的攻击性行为需要受过训练的人员持续看护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 combative</td><td>/kəmˈbætɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，好争辩的</td><td>a combative tone<br>好斗的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered every question in a combative tone.  他用好斗的语气回答每一个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 militant</td><td>/ˈmɪlətənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>激进好战的；激进分子</td><td>a militant activist<br>激进的活动人士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union took a militant stance in the pay talks.  工会在薪资谈判中采取了强硬立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pugnacious</td><td>/pʌɡˈneɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，爱争吵的</td><td>a pugnacious debater<br>好争辩的辩手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pugnacious little dog barked at every passing car.  这只好斗的小狗对每辆过路的车都叫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 truculent</td><td>/ˈtrʌkjələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，好斗的；粗野的</td><td>a truculent response<br>咄咄逼人的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truculent teenager argued with every teacher who tried to correct him.  这个好斗的少年跟每位试图纠正他的老师都吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonaggressive</del></td><td>/ˌnɑːnəˈɡresɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好斗的，温和的</td><td>a nonaggressive approach<br>不具攻击性的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog is large but entirely nonaggressive with children.  这条狗很大，但对孩子完全不具攻击性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonbelligerent</del></td><td>/ˌnɑːnbəˈlɪdʒərənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>非交战的，不好战的</td><td>a nonbelligerent nation<br>非交战国</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty guaranteed the island's nonbelligerent status during the whole conflict.  条约保证了该岛在整场冲突期间的非交战国地位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacific</td><td>/pəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平和的，爱好和平的</td><td>a pacific temperament<br>平和的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He settled the quarrel with a few pacific words.  他用几句平和的话平息了争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peaceful</td><td>/ˈpiːsfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和平的，平静的</td><td>a peaceful settlement<br>和平的解决方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides finally chose a peaceful path out of conflict.  双方最终选择了走出冲突的和平道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unbelligerent</del></td><td>/ˌʌnbəˈlɪdʒərənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好战的，不挑事的</td><td>an unbelligerent tone<br>不挑事的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unbelligerent reply cooled the whole argument.  他不挑事的回答让整场争论冷了下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncombative</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkəmˈbætɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好斗的，不爱争的</td><td>an uncombative reply<br>不带火气的回复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her uncombative answer surprised the hostile interviewer and calmed the room.  她不带火气的回答让不友好的采访者意外，也让场面缓和下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timorous</td><td>/ˈtɪmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的</td><td>a timorous little voice<br>胆怯的小声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The timorous student finally raised her hand to answer the question.  那个胆怯的学生终于举手回答问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scrap</b></td><td>/skræp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抛弃</td><td>scrap the whole plan<br>废弃整个计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Managers decided to scrap the whole plan and start again from scratch.  管理层决定废弃整个计划，从头再来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ditch</td><td>/ˈdɪtʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>抛弃，摆脱；沟渠</td><td>ditch the old plan<br>放弃旧计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team ditched the old design after two failed tests.  两次测试失败后，团队放弃了旧设计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dump</td><td>/dʌmp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>丢弃，倾倒；抛弃</td><td>dump the waste<br>倾倒废弃物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Factories once dumped chemical waste straight into the river.  工厂曾把化学废料直接倾倒进河里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jettison</td><td>/ˈdʒetɪsn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抛弃，放弃（想法、计划等）</td><td>jettison the proposal<br>放弃该提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company jettisoned the proposal as soon as funding became uncertain.  资金一变得不确定，公司就放弃了这项提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 junk</td><td>/dʒʌŋk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>废旧杂物，垃圾</td><td>a garage full of junk<br>满是废物的车库</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He finally cleared the junk out of his crowded garage.  他终于把拥挤车库里的废物清理掉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toss</td><td>/ˈtɔːs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>扔，抛；抛弃</td><td>toss aside<br>随手丢在一边</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She tossed the crumpled letter into the fire without reading it.  她没读就把那封皱信扔进了火里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 throw away</td><td>/θroʊ əˈweɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>扔掉，浪费</td><td>throw away the packaging<br>扔掉包装</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not throw away the receipt; you may need it.  别扔掉收据，你可能会用到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 retrieve</td><td>/rɪˈtriːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>寻回，找回</td><td>retrieve lost data<br>找回丢失的数据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The engineer managed to retrieve the files from the damaged hard drive.  工程师成功从损坏的硬盘中找回了文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scrawl</b></td><td>/skrɔːl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>乱涂，潦草地写</td><td>scrawl a note<br>潦草地写张便条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He had to scrawl a quick note before running for the train.  他赶火车前只能潦草地写了张便条。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scratch</td><td>/ˈskrætʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>刮擦，抓挠；擦痕</td><td>scratch the surface<br>刮擦表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat scratched the wooden door all night long.  猫整夜抓挠着木门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squiggle</td><td>/ˈskwɪɡəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>曲线，弯曲的涂画</td><td>a squiggle on the page<br>纸上的一道弯曲涂画</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His signature is just a squiggle at the bottom.  他的签名只是底部的一道弯线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scribble</td><td>/ˈskrɪbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>潦草地书写，乱写</td><td>scribble in the margin<br>在页边潦草乱写</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The student began to scribble notes in the margin of the textbook.  那名学生开始在教科书页边潦草地写笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 write carefully</td><td>/raɪt ˈkɛrfəli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>仔细工整地书写</td><td>write carefully in ink<br>用墨水仔细书写</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please write carefully so the clerk can read your name.  请仔细书写，让职员能看清你的名字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scribble</b></td><td>/ˈskrɪbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>潦草地书写，乱写</td><td>scribble in the margin<br>在页边潦草乱写</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The student began to scribble notes in the margin of the textbook.  那名学生开始在教科书页边潦草地写笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scratch</td><td>/ˈskrætʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>刮擦，抓挠；擦痕</td><td>scratch the surface<br>刮擦表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat scratched the wooden door all night long.  猫整夜抓挠着木门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scrawl</td><td>/skrɔːl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>乱涂，潦草地写</td><td>scrawl a note<br>潦草地写张便条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He had to scrawl a quick note before running for the train.  他赶火车前只能潦草地写了张便条。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squiggle</td><td>/ˈskwɪɡəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>曲线，弯曲的涂画</td><td>a squiggle on the page<br>纸上的一道弯曲涂画</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His signature is just a squiggle at the bottom.  他的签名只是底部的一道弯线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scrupulous</b></td><td>/ˈskruːpjələs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的；一丝不苟的</td><td>scrupulous honesty<br>一丝不苟的诚实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her scrupulous honesty made her the obvious choice to handle the accounts.  她一丝不苟的诚实使她成为管账的当然人选。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 conscionable</del></td><td>/ˈkɑnʃənəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合乎良心的，正当的</td><td>a conscionable agreement<br>一份公道的协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No conscionable judge would enforce so one-sided a contract.  有良心的法官不会强制执行如此偏袒一方的合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ethical</td><td>/ˈeθɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>道德的，合乎道义的</td><td>ethical standards<br>道德标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters must weigh ethical limits before publishing private letters.  记者发表私人信件前必须权衡道德界限。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 honest</td><td>/ˈɑːnəst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>诚实的，正直的</td><td>an honest answer<br>一个诚实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me an honest answer, even if it disappoints me.  给我一个诚实的答复，即使会让我失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 honorable</td><td>/ˈɑːnərəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>光荣的，可敬的</td><td>an honorable retreat<br>光荣的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made the honorable choice and returned all the money.  她做出可敬的选择，把钱全退了回去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moral</td><td>/ˈmɔːrəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>道德的；道德准则</td><td>a moral obligation<br>道义上的义务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They felt a moral obligation to shelter the refugees.  他们觉得有道义上的义务收留难民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 principled</td><td>/ˈprɪnsəpəld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有原则的，有道德的</td><td>a principled stand<br>有原则的立场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her principled refusal cost her a promising position.  她基于原则的拒绝让她失去了一个有前途的职位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careful</td><td>/ˈkerfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，仔细的</td><td>careful consideration<br>慎重考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be careful when you cross this busy street at night.  夜里穿过这条繁忙的街道时要小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conscientious</td><td>/ˌkɑːnʃiˈenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>仔细的，一丝不苟的；有良心的，正直的</td><td>a conscientious worker<br>一位认真的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A conscientious editor checks every fact before the article goes to print.  一丝不苟的编辑会在文章付印前核实每一个事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fussy</td><td>/ˈfʌsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，过分讲究的</td><td>fussy about small details<br>对小细节很挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old tailor is fussy about the smallest details.  这位老裁缝对最小的细节都很挑剔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meticulous</td><td>/məˈtɪkjələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一丝不苟的，极为细致的</td><td>meticulous attention to detail<br>对细节极为在意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restorer's meticulous attention to detail saved the fragile manuscript from further damage.  修复师一丝不苟地关注细节，使这份脆弱的手稿免于进一步损坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cutthroat</td><td>/ˈkʌtˌθroʊt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>残酷无情的</td><td>cutthroat competition<br>残酷的竞争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market is cutthroat, and small shops rarely survive.  这个市场竞争残酷，小店很少能生存。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dishonest</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnəst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不诚实的，欺骗的</td><td>a dishonest business practice<br>不诚实的商业做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company used dishonest advertising to attract careless customers.  公司用不诚实的广告吸引不细心的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dishonorable</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnərəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不光彩的，可耻的</td><td>a dishonorable discharge<br>不光彩的除役</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Betraying an old friend for money is plainly dishonorable.  为钱背叛老友显然可耻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immoral</td><td>/ɪˈmɔːrəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不道德的</td><td>an immoral bargain<br>不道德的交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many considered the deal immoral even if it was legal.  许多人认为这笔交易即使合法也不道德。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unconscionable</td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːnʃənəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分的，不合理的；不道德的</td><td>unconscionable delay<br>过分的拖延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Charging that fee for a five-minute repair seems unconscionable to me.  五分钟的维修收那笔费用在我看来太过分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unethical</td><td>/ʌnˈeθɪkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合道德准则的</td><td>unethical business practices<br>不合道德的商业做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hiding the test results was clearly unethical.  隐瞒检测结果显然不合道德。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unjust</td><td>/ʌnˈdʒʌst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不公正的，不公平的</td><td>an unjust punishment<br>不公正的惩罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many considered the sentence unjust and demanded a new trial.  许多人认为判决不公，要求重审。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unprincipled</td><td>/ʌnˈprɪnsəpəld/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无原则的，无良的</td><td>an unprincipled politician<br>毫无原则的政客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unprincipled agent sold the same house twice.  这个无良中介把同一栋房子卖了两次。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>scrutable</b></del></td><td>/ˈskruːtəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可以理解的</td><td>scrutable motives<br>可探明的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few of his decisions were scrutable to the junior staff.  他的决定很少能被初级员工理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accessible</td><td>/əkˈsesəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可以到达的；可理解的</td><td>an accessible explanation<br>易懂的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He makes physics accessible to ordinary readers.  他让普通读者也能理解物理学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 apprehensible</del></td><td>/ˌæprɪˈhensəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的</td><td>readily apprehensible ideas<br>易于理解的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His argument is barely apprehensible to readers without training.  他的论证对没有受过训练的读者几乎难以理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comprehensible</td><td>/ˌkɑːmpriˈhensəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的，易懂的</td><td>clear and comprehensible<br>清晰易懂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers make difficult ideas comprehensible to complete beginners.  好老师能让初学者也理解艰深的概念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 fathomable</del></td><td>/ˈfæðəməbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的，可探明的</td><td>hardly fathomable motives<br>难以捉摸的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her reasons for leaving were not easily fathomable to us.  她离开的原因我们不容易理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 legible</td><td>/ˈledʒəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>字迹清楚的，易辨认的</td><td>barely legible handwriting<br>几乎难以辨认的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His handwriting is so small that it is hardly legible.  他的字太小，几乎难以辨认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mysterious</td><td>/mɪˈstɪriəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>a mysterious stranger<br>神秘的陌生人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A mysterious light appeared over the lake around midnight.  午夜时分湖面上出现了一道神秘的光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incoherent</td><td>/ˌɪnkoʊˈhɪrənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>语无伦次的，不连贯的</td><td>an incoherent argument<br>前后不连贯的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was so upset that his explanation became incoherent.  他太难受了，解释变得语无伦次。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incomprehensible</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːmprɪˈhensəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法理解的，难懂的</td><td>utterly incomprehensible instructions<br>完全无法理解的说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The instructions were incomprehensible to a first-time user.  这些说明对第一次使用的人来说无法理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inscrutable</td><td>/ɪnˈskruːtəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高深莫测的，难以理解的</td><td>an inscrutable expression<br>高深莫测的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inscrutable smile gave nothing away about his plans.  他那高深莫测的笑容丝毫没有透露他的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insensible</td><td>/ɪnˈsensəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知觉的，昏迷的</td><td>insensible to pain<br>感觉不到疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blow left him insensible for several minutes.  这一击使他几分钟内失去了知觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scrutinize</b></td><td>/ˈskruːtənaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>仔细审查；细看</td><td>scrutinize the document<br>仔细审查文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Auditors will scrutinize every receipt before approving the annual budget report.  审计员会在批准年度预算报告前仔细审查每一张收据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 examine</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæmɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>细查，仔细审视</td><td>examine the evidence closely<br>仔细审查证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scientist examined every slide under the microscope twice.  这位科学家把每张切片在显微镜下看了两遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 review</td><td>/rɪˈvjuː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>审查，复审</td><td>review the evidence<br>审查证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee will review each application in detail.  委员会将详细审查每份申请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scan</td><td>/ˈskæn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>细看；浏览，扫视</td><td>scan the horizon<br>扫视地平线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He scanned the horizon for any sign of the missing boat.  他扫视地平线，寻找失踪小船的踪影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 survey</td><td>/sərˈveɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>调查；勘测；概览</td><td>conduct a survey<br>开展一项调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We surveyed every household about their water use last summer.  去年夏天我们调查了每户人家的用水情况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plumb</td><td>/plʌm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>完全的；深入探究，探测</td><td>plumb the depths of<br>深入探究…的深处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The biographer tried to plumb the depths of that private grief.  传记作者试图深入探究那份私人悲伤的深处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 check</td><td>/tʃek/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使突然停止，阻止；检查，核对</td><td>check the spread of disease<br>阻止疾病蔓延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict quarantine measures checked the spread of the disease.  严格的隔离措施阻止了疾病的蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 casually glance</td><td>/ˈkæʒuəli ˈɡlæns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>随意扫一眼</td><td>casually glance at a headline<br>随意扫一眼标题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He would casually glance at the report without reading it.  他会随意扫一眼报告，却不细读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gloss over</td><td>/ɡlɔs ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>掩饰，含糊带过</td><td>gloss over the problems<br>对问题轻描淡写</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report glossed over the losses of the previous year.  报告对前一年的亏损轻描淡写。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scuff</b></td><td>/skʌf/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）磨损</td><td>scuff one's shoes<br>磨损鞋子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He scuffed the toes of his new shoes on the steps.  他在台阶上把新鞋头磨花了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abrade</td><td>/əˈbreɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>磨损，擦伤；使烦躁</td><td>abrade the skin<br>擦伤皮肤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rough rope abraded the sailor's palms.  粗糙的绳子磨伤了水手的手掌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 graze</td><td>/ˈɡreɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>擦伤，轻擦；放牧</td><td>graze one's knee<br>擦伤膝盖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She fell and grazed her knee on the gravel.  她摔倒了，膝盖在碎石上擦伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scratch</td><td>/ˈskrætʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>刮擦，抓挠；擦痕</td><td>scratch the surface<br>刮擦表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat scratched the wooden door all night long.  猫整夜抓挠着木门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scurrilous</b></td><td>/ˈskɜːrələs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶语中伤的，下流的</td><td>scurrilous accusations<br>下流的指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet contained scurrilous attacks on every member of the council.  那本小册子对议会每位成员都有下流的攻击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 contumelious</del></td><td>/ˌkɑntjuˈmiliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢无礼的，辱骂的</td><td>contumelious remarks<br>傲慢辱骂的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His contumelious tone offended nearly everyone at the meeting.  他傲慢无礼的语气几乎冒犯了会上所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 invective</td><td>/ɪnˈvektɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>谩骂，痛骂；侮辱性的</td><td>a stream of invective<br>一连串的谩骂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The politician's invective attacks on his rival shocked even his own supporters.  这位政客对对手的侮辱性攻击甚至震惊了他自己的支持者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 opprobrious</del></td><td>/əˈproʊbriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辱骂的；臭名昭著的，可耻的</td><td>opprobrious remarks<br>辱骂性的言辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet was filled with opprobrious attacks on the mayor.  那本小册子充满了对市长的辱骂性攻击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 truculent</td><td>/ˈtrʌkjələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，好斗的；粗野的</td><td>a truculent response<br>咄咄逼人的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truculent teenager argued with every teacher who tried to correct him.  这个好斗的少年跟每位试图纠正他的老师都吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vitriolic</td><td>/ˌvɪtriˈɑːlɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言辞）刻薄的</td><td>a vitriolic attack<br>尖刻的攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review was so vitriolic that the author stopped writing.  那篇评论如此刻薄，作者因此停笔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vituperative</td><td>/ˌvaɪˈtuːpərətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谩骂的，斥责的</td><td>a vituperative attack<br>谩骂式的攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His vituperative letter to the editor was never printed.  他写给编辑的那封谩骂信从未刊出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scurvy</b></td><td>/ˈskɜːrvi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>下流的，让人鄙视的</td><td>a scurvy trick<br>下流的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheating an old friend was a scurvy trick nobody could excuse.  欺骗老友是种下流手段，谁也无法原谅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deplorable</td><td>/dɪˈplɔːrəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>糟糕的，恶劣的；可鄙的</td><td>deplorable working conditions<br>恶劣的工作条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors found deplorable working conditions inside the crowded garment factory.  检查人员在拥挤的服装厂内发现了恶劣的工作条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despicable</td><td>/dɪˈspɪkəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人鄙视的</td><td>a despicable act<br>令人鄙视的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stealing from a charity is a despicable way to make money.  从慈善机构偷钱是一种令人鄙视的赚钱方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grubby</td><td>/ˈɡrʌbi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的；卑劣的</td><td>grubby hands<br>脏兮兮的手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy came home with grubby knees and a torn shirt.  男孩回家时膝盖脏兮兮，衬衫也破了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lousy</td><td>/ˈlaʊzi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多得很的，充满的；糟糕的</td><td>lousy with tourists<br>游客多得很</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In August the town is lousy with tourists from every corner of Europe.  八月这座小镇挤满了来自欧洲各地的游客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scummy</td><td>/ˈskʌmi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣的，肮脏的</td><td>a scummy landlord<br>卑劣的房东</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That was a scummy way to treat an old friend.  那样对待老朋友太卑劣了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wretched</td><td>/ˈretʃɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极差的；沮丧的</td><td>wretched living conditions<br>恶劣的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Families lived in wretched conditions beside the flooded riverbank.  一些家庭在泛滥的河岸边过着极差的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 admirable</td><td>/ˈædmərəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人钦佩的；极好的</td><td>admirable courage<br>令人钦佩的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her patience with difficult students was truly admirable.  她对难教学生的耐心确实令人钦佩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commendable</td><td>/kəˈmendəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>commendable honesty<br>值得赞扬的诚实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rescue team showed commendable calm during the long night.  救援队在漫长的夜里表现出值得赞扬的镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 creditable</td><td>/ˈkredətəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得称赞的；相当好的</td><td>a creditable performance<br>值得称赞的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young violinist gave a creditable account of the concerto.  年轻小提琴手对协奏曲的演绎值得称赞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laudable</td><td>/ˈlɔːdəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>a laudable effort<br>值得赞扬的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her attempt to save the library was laudable though it failed.  她拯救图书馆的尝试虽失败却值得赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meritorious</td><td>/ˌmerəˈtɔːriəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有功的，值得称赞的</td><td>meritorious service<br>有功的服务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He received a medal for meritorious conduct under heavy fire.  他因在猛烈炮火下的有功表现获得奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praiseworthy</td><td>/ˈpreɪzˌwɜːrði/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>a praiseworthy effort<br>值得赞扬的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their decision to return the money was entirely praiseworthy.  他们归还钱款的决定完全值得赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>seamy</b></td><td>/ˈsiːmi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的，堕落的</td><td>the seamy side of life<br>生活的阴暗面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel explores the seamy side of life in the port city.  这部小说探讨了那座港口城市生活的阴暗面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sordid</td><td>/ˈsɔːrdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的，不干净的；卑鄙的</td><td>a sordid little room<br>肮脏的小房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lived for years in a sordid little room without heat.  他们在没有暖气的肮脏小屋里住了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 base</td><td>/ˈbeɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣的，低下的</td><td>base motives<br>卑劣的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He acted from base motives of greed and envy.  他出于贪婪和嫉妒的卑劣动机行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decent and respectable</td><td>/ˈdiːsənt ənd rɪˈspɛktəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>正派体面的</td><td>a decent and respectable family<br>正派体面的家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lived a decent and respectable life in that town.  他们在那座小镇过着正派体面的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>secluded</b></td><td>/sɪˈkluːdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>僻静的，隐蔽的</td><td>a secluded cottage<br>一间僻静的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They honeymooned in a secluded valley far from any road.  他们在远离公路的僻静山谷度蜜月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cloistered</td><td>/ˈklɔɪstərd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐居的，与世隔绝的</td><td>a cloistered life<br>隐居的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a cloistered life among books, rarely meeting strangers.  他在书堆中过着与世隔绝的生活，很少见陌生人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 covert</td><td>/ˈkoʊvərt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐蔽的，秘密的</td><td>covert operations<br>秘密行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The agency ran covert operations in several neighboring countries.  该机构在数个邻国进行秘密行动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 isolated</td><td>/ˈaɪsəˌleɪtəd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>偏僻的，孤立的</td><td>an isolated farmhouse<br>偏僻的农舍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabin sits on an isolated ridge above the lake.  小屋坐落在湖上一处偏僻的山脊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
